# Claims digest (201 files, 479 claims, 201 with breadth)

## 1introduction-2
- [supports] UAB reallocates a fixed sampling budget across questions using vote entropy of the first K samples with a marginal-greedy solution to a concave coverage surrogate, improving average accuracy by +2.3% over uniform allocation and up to +5.5% on individual benchmarks. | target: L3: uncertainty-routed budgets exist for math/logic via sampled consistency | scope: Five open models 1.5B-27B, five reasoning benchmarks, no code
- [supports] Against an oracle (smallest sample count whose majority vote is correct) at N=4 on Qwen2.5-1.5B, vote-entropy UAB (K=2) leaves 10.5% of questions under-allocated versus 13.3% for Uniform and 14.1% for an oracle-difficulty variant. | target: allocation quality vs oracle and uniform | scope: Table 5, mean over five datasets, excluding questions unsolved in 16 samples
- [supports] Logit-based signals (NLL, Max Token NLL, ANLL) give allocation accuracies of 44.8-45.0 average, below vote entropy 47.0, indicating sampled disagreement is the stronger routing signal in this setting. | target: sampled > logit for routing (math) | scope: Table 4 with Qwen2.5-1.5B, N=4
- [neutral] UAB uses no auxiliary model and no verbalized confidence or hidden-state probe, and contains no code benchmark; it is related to L3 by design but does not test elicited confidence or execution ground truth. | target: L3 narrowness | scope: Intro; full-text grep verbalized 0, HumanEval 0, LiveCodeBench 0
  breadth: confidence_families=Vote entropy of K initial samples (answer disagreement); baselines NLL, Max-token NLL, ANLL, length, random, uniform (Table 4-5); model_families_and_sizes=Five open-weight models 1.5B-27B (e.g. Qwen2.5-1.5B) (Abstract); base_vs_instruct=not present (searched: not studied); post_training_stages=not present (searched: not studied); reasoning_models=not present (searched: reasoning-model comparison; benchmarks are 'reasoning' tasks but models are standard open-weight); elicitation_formats=Sampled-consistency (vote entropy) vs logit NLL; verbalized: 0 hits; benchmarks=MATH500, Formal Logic, DeepScaler, GSM8K, Graded Arithmetic (Table 4); no code (HumanEval/LiveCodeBench/MBPP 0 hits); label_source=Answer match with majority vote correctness; label_independence=Oracle(q) = smallest sample count whose majority vote is correct (Table 5); contamination_split=not present (searched: none); calibration_metrics=Accuracy at N=4; %Under/%Over-C/%Over-W vs oracle (Table 5); no ECE; granularity=Per question; causal_intervention=not present (searched: none)

## 1introduction-3
- [supports] Staged verification (lightweight filters then ORM ranking) shifts the accuracy-throughput Pareto frontier outward, giving 11.64x higher throughput at 8.26% accuracy loss versus full test-suite verification. | target: verification-budget routing to cheaper signals | scope: Qwen 2.5 Coder Instruct generators, four Python benchmarks, best-of-64
- [neutral] Results are averaged over six seeds with 95% CIs; median CI width 1.48 percentage points. | target: statistical reporting | scope: Table 1-2
- [neutral] Both generators and ORM backbones are from the Qwen family, which may favor the ORM through distribution matching. | target: limitation | scope: Section on training setup
- [supports] Test-based filtering catches bugs the ORM misses: filtered candidates sat 31 ranks too high on average. | target: learned scorers vs execution | scope: Section on filtering analysis
  breadth: confidence_families=Outcome reward models (learned Bradley-Terry-style scorer), majority voting, syntax/lint/N-test filters as verification signals (Section 3-4, Table 1). No verbal or hidden-state confidence of the generator.; model_families_and_sizes=Qwen 2.5 Coder Instruct generators (7B training candidates; sizes 1.5B-14B in analyses) and Qwen-family ORM backbones (training section).; base_vs_instruct=not present as a pair (Instruct generators only); searched: base.; post_training_stages=ORM trained 2 epochs with margin objective on pairs (4.7K CodeContests and 7.3K GSM8K problems); no RLHF of generators.; reasoning_models=not present (searched: reasoning model, thinking); elicitation_formats=Best-of-64 sampling at temperatures with ORM ranking and staged filtering (Section 3-4).; benchmarks=Four Python benchmarks per Abstract; those named in text: CodeContests (training pairs 4.7K plus evaluation, LiveCodeBench-style STDIN format noted at Section on datasets), GSM8K (training pairs 7.3K), HumanEval and MBPP via EvalPlus (distribution-shift evaluation). The fourth benchmark is not identified in the text I read.; label_source=Test-suite execution (full suite as strong verifier).; label_independence=Distribution-shift evaluation on HumanEval and MBPP separate from CodeContests/GSM8K training problems (training section); six seeds.; contamination_split=not present (searched: contamination, release date; only distribution-shift split described); calibration_metrics=not present (searched: ECE, Brier, calibration error; evaluation is accuracy (best-of-64) and throughput PPS); granularity=Per-candidate program scoring; per-test pruning (N Test filters with first N tests).; causal_intervention=Filter ablations (syntax, lint, N tests) and temperature ablations (Tables 6-10); no model-internal intervention.

## 1introduction
- [supports] On HumanEval with three ~1-3B instruct code models, single-forward-pass estimators (mean token entropy, verbalized confidence, entropy ensemble, semantic entropy probe) correlate only weakly with correctness while multi-sample verifier-based P(True) correlates strongly. | target: L2: hidden-state probes do not beat sampled readouts on small code models (counter-evidence to probe-beats-verbal) | scope: HumanEval, Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct / Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct / Deepseek-Coder-1.3B-Instruct, Pearson correlation, Table 1
- [neutral] Verifier-based P(True) here is defined as the fraction of K sampled solutions that pass tests, i.e. it uses execution as the confidence signal, so its high correlation is partly label-leakage-like rather than a self-assessed confidence. | target: L3/L1: definition of the strongest readout | scope: Section 3.2.3
- [supports] Uncertainty-triggered regeneration degrades Pass@1 in 5 of 6 configurations (-3 pp to -10 pp) while verification-based regeneration improves Pass@1 (+6 to +26 pp HumanEval, +8 to +20 pp BigCodeBench); authors conclude uncertainty is best a gate for costlier execution loops. | target: L3: uncertainty-gated execution budgets; closest occupant on routing-to-execution, but gate is weak SEP and policy is regeneration not budget allocation | scope: HumanEval held-out n=31, Table 2; abstract
- [neutral] Evaluation is small: held-out set for self-correction is 31 HumanEval problems, thresholds tuned by grid search on a HumanEval validation split optimizing F1; text names DeepSeek-R1 in Section 3.4 while Table 1 lists Deepseek-Coder-1.3B-Instruct. | target: evidence strength of the occupant | scope: Sections 3.3.1, 3.4, Table 2 caption
  breadth: confidence_families=token entropy, verbalized confidence, P(True) (verifier-based), entropy ensemble over prompts, semantic entropy probes SLT/TBG (Sec 3.2); model_families_and_sizes=Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct, Deepseek-Coder-1.3B-Instruct (Table 1); Sec 3.4 says LLaMA 3, Qwen2.5-Coder, DeepSeek-R1 ~3B instruct variants; base_vs_instruct=instruct only; no base counterparts (Table 1); post_training_stages=not present (searched: base/SFT/RL comparison); reasoning_models=Sec 3.4 mentions DeepSeek-R1 but Table 1 uses Deepseek-Coder-1.3B-Instruct; reasoning mode not stated; elicitation_formats=verbalized 'Confidence: c' in [0,1] parsed by pattern (Sec 3.2.2); probe at second-to-last token (SLT) or token-before-generation (TBG); benchmarks=HumanEval, BigCodeBench (Sec 3.4); label_source=unit-test pass/fail via verifiers library (Sec 3.1-3.2); label_independence=SEP label is semantic entropy from symbolic-execution clustering of samples, not test outcome (Sec 3.2.5); contamination_split=not present (searched: release-date, contamination); thresholds tuned on a HumanEval validation split, evaluated on n=31 held-out; calibration_metrics=Pearson correlation with pass/fail and calibration plots (Sec 3.4); no ECE/Brier/AUROC reported; granularity=whole-solution (function) level; causal_intervention=self-correction policies gated on uncertainty (Sec 3.3, Table 2); no activation-level intervention

## 260305544-yates-covariance-decomposition-rearrangement-for-brier-score-full-text
- [supports] Brier score equals a sum of three nonnegative terms: variance mismatch, covariance (correlation) deficit, and calibration-in-the-large. | target: measurement-instrument decomposition of Brier score (F) | scope: binary outcomes, any forecaster

## abstract
- [supports] Static single-token hidden-state probes reach 0.90 AUROC and 0.96 F1 on LiveCodeBench in the best settings across four open-weight instruct LLMs, and richer dynamic token-selection or sequence models give no consistent gain. | target: hidden states encode code correctness (L2 probe occupancy) | scope: see summary
- [supports] Line-level fault localization with known-incorrect programs reaches a Top-3 hit rate of 81% in the best setting, while mixed-program line-level prediction is substantially harder. | target: L4 localization occupancy (hidden-state, not per-requirement) | scope: see summary
- [supports] The thesis forgoes strict contamination control: models were released after the LiveCodeBench cutoff and the author acknowledges possible contamination. | target: L1 release-date contamination control is not done here | scope: see summary
- [supports] A Qwen3-Coder single-token probe attains the highest Brier Skill Score on the CALIBRI test split, above the multicalibration baselines reported by Campos et al., but only for one model and one external benchmark. | target: probe beats post-hoc calibration on code | scope: see summary
- [neutral] Model table gives release date and knowledge cutoff per model (Qwen3-Coder Jul 31, 2025 / Nov 20, 2024; GPT-OSS-20B Aug 5, 2025 / Jun 1, 2024; Nemotron-3-Nano Dec 15, 2025 / Nov 25, 2025; Olmo 3.1 Instruct Nov 20, 2025 / Dec 1, 2024), but does not split tasks by date. | target: L1 contamination split | scope: see summary
  breadth: confidence_families=hidden-state probes (static single-token MLP/XGBoost, LSTM, entropy-filtered token selection); mentions verbalized and sampling UE only in background (Sec 3.2); Brier Skill Score vs multicalibration baselines (Sec 5.2.3); model_families_and_sizes=Qwen3-Coder (48 layers), GPT-OSS-20B, NVIDIA-Nemotron-3-Nano, Olmo 3.1 Instruct 32B (Table 4.2); base_vs_instruct=instruct only (Sec 4.2 'Four open-weight, instruction-tuned LLMs'); no base models; post_training_stages=not present (searched: base, SFT, RLHF stage comparison); reasoning_models=GPT-OSS-20B is a reasoning-capable model; no reasoning on/off comparison found; elicitation_formats=LCB zero-shot benchmark prompt; BCB Instruct vs Complete prompt variants (Sec 4.1.2); no verbal confidence prompt formats; benchmarks=LiveCodeBench code synthesis (1055 tasks), BigCodeBench (1,140 tasks), CALIBRI split (Table 5.7); label_source=benchmark test-suite execution (avg 17 tests per LCB problem); fix-then-diff LLM repair for line labels (Sec 4.3); label_independence=not present (searched: independence of label from probe training split by problem; per-problem grouped split not confirmed); contamination_split=explicitly not controlled: Sec 4.1.1 acknowledges possible contamination; release/cutoff dates listed in Table 4.2 but no date split; calibration_metrics=AUROC, F1, Brier Skill Score (Sec 5.1 metrics); ECE term appears in text but BSS is the calibration metric reported; granularity=response-level and line-level (token and line labels via fix-then-diff); causal_intervention=not present (searched: steering, ablation) ; only 'ablat' appears in 4 passing mentions

## aces-who-tests-the-tests-leave-one-outauc-consistency-for-code-generation
- [supports] Test correctness can be bypassed: a held-out test's pass/fail pattern is scored for agreement (LOO-AUC) with the ranking induced by all remaining tests, and expected LOO-AUC is proportional to the test's discriminative power. | target: L4 self-written test adequacy has a formal occupant | scope: see summary
- [supports] Pass@k with test weights w is lower-bounded by 1 - (n^-/k) exp(-R(w)/2) where R(w) is discriminative power. | target: test-weighting theory | scope: see summary
- [neutral] Evaluation uses HumanEval (164), HumanEval+ (164), MBPP (427) with GPT-3.5-Turbo candidates and tests from Huang et al. (2024), about 200 candidates and 500 tests per problem. | target: breadth of the ACES evaluation | scope: see summary
  breadth: confidence_families=test-weight scores from the binary pass matrix (LOO-AUC); no model confidence; model_families_and_sizes=GPT-3.5-Turbo candidates; GPT-4, DeepSeek-Coder, CodeLlama as direct-inference baselines (Table 2); base_vs_instruct=not present (searched: base, instruct); post_training_stages=not present; reasoning_models=not present; elicitation_formats=not present (searched: verbal confidence); benchmarks=HumanEval, HumanEval+, MBPP (Sec Experiments); label_source=ground-truth tests of each benchmark; LLM-generated tests used as signals; label_independence=selection signal uses generated tests, labels use benchmark tests; contamination_split=not present (searched: contamination, LiveCodeBench); calibration_metrics=Pass@k; no ECE/Brier; granularity=per-test weights over candidates (test-level, not requirement-level); causal_intervention=not present

## an-empirical-study-of-security-calibration-in-large-language-models-for-code
- [neutral] Three API/hosted models are overconfident on security correctness of generated code; verbalized-confidence ECE is 0.456-0.481 (GPT-4o-mini), 0.408-0.421 (Qwen3-Coder-Next), 0.247-0.263 (Gemini-2.0-Flash) across six temperatures. | target: R1 candidate occupancy | scope: Sec. V-A, Table I
- [neutral] Functional-correctness calibration is worse than security calibration; compared on the same four elicitation methods with execution-test labels. | target: R1 candidate occupancy | scope: Abstract; Sec. V-A1
- [neutral] Token-probability confidence yields ECE about 0.80, sampling-consistency near-zero ECE only because it is prompt-level, self-consistency about 0.07 at higher temperatures; verbalized judged most practical. | target: R1 candidate occupancy | scope: Sec. V (elicitation comparison)
- [neutral] Calibration-guided closed-loop repair (isotonic/logistic thresholds) yields limited improvement and frequent functional regressions; execution gating improves calibration post hoc on the passing subset. Repair is whole-sample, security-focused, not per-requirement. | target: R1 candidate occupancy | scope: RQ3/RQ4, Abstract

## assessing-correctness-in-llm-based-code-generation-via-uncertainty-estimation
- [supports] Semantic entropy and mutual information with embedding or CodeBLEU clustering, and token log-probabilities, do not significantly correlate with code correctness on LiveCodeBench. | target: L1: negative result for transferred NLG UQ on code | scope: gpt-3.5-turbo-instruct, DeepSeek-R1, claude-3-7-sonnet; LiveCodeBench 831 problems
- [supports] Symbolic-execution clustering restores significant correlation, and uniform-distribution estimates perform comparably, so token probabilities are not essential. | target: L1 | scope: Python, bounded loop unrolling
- [supports] Symbolic cluster count used as an abstention threshold achieves a false positive rate below 0.02%. | target: L3: abstention with a sampled signal on code is OCCUPIED (abstention, not execution-budget routing) | scope: LiveCodeBench; Python

## certified-against-which-oracleexecution-labels-set-the-reported-riskof-conformal
- [neutral] Swapping shipped-database execution labels for the multi-instance test suite raises held-out conformal risk by 2.73 to 10.23 points above the risk reported under the original labels (four SQL checkpoints, two split schemes). | target: R1 candidate occupancy | scope: Abstract
- [neutral] Every execution-consistency confidence score looks better under the labels of the oracle that built its clusters, in 16 of 16 combinations; under expert labels, suite clusters raise AUROC by 6.96 and 1.53 points. | target: R1 candidate occupancy | scope: Abstract
- [neutral] Under blinded expert labels a certificate calibrated at nominal 0.10 carries 20.0 and 17.2 points of risk on two checkpoints. | target: R1 candidate occupancy | scope: Abstract
- [neutral] Recommendation: evaluate a consistency score under an oracle that did not build it; report certificates under both oracles. | target: R1 candidate occupancy | scope: Abstract

## characterizing-overconfident-failure-in-llm-based-code-generation-full-text
- [supports] Across four 7-8B instruct code models and HumanEval+/MBPP+, no uncertainty metric exceeds AUC 0.70 for pass/fail discrimination. | target: token-level uncertainty is a weak proxy for execution correctness in code LLMs | scope: four 7-8B instruct models; HumanEval+ and MBPP+; BigCodeBench excluded from Sec 4
- [supports] Platt scaling and isotonic regression sharply reduce Brier score but give Brier Skill Score near zero, i.e. no skill beyond the benchmark pass rate. | target: calibration metrics can look good without discrimination in code | scope: MBPP+ and BigCodeBench, 5-fold stratified CV (Sec 6.1, Table 5)
- [supports] Instruction tuning increases certainty on failing generations without reliably improving pass/fail discrimination (OpenCoder dAUC -0.0136, CI [-0.0911, 0.0652]; DeepSeek-Coder AUC 0.4391 to 0.5654). | target: base vs instruct comparison for code confidence is partially occupied at token level | scope: OpenCoder and DeepSeek-Coder on MBPP+ (Sec 5.5, Fig 4)
- [supports] Prompted abstention does not reliably induce deferral in code LLMs even with an explicit IDK option. | target: verbal/prompted readouts are weak in code | scope: qualitative across the four subject models (Sec 6.3, Fig 6)
- [neutral] Hidden-state effective rank differs between passing and failing generations in middle and later layers, offered only as exploratory evidence; no trained probe, no verbal comparison. | target: L2 probe-vs-verbal on code: this paper is exploratory at the latent level, not a probe-vs-verbal comparison | scope: CodeLlama and QwenCoder on HumanEval+ (Sec 7, Fig 7)
- [neutral] Authors state the scope limits: four open 7-8B models, three benchmarks, white-box access, test-suite labels approximate correctness. | target: scope of this occupant | scope: Sec 10 threats to validity
  breadth: confidence_families=token confidence/entropy and 17 generation-stage plus 6 prefill-stage uncertainty scores (Sec 4, Tables 2-3); Platt/isotonic post-hoc calibration (Sec 6.1); prompted abstention 'I don't know' (Sec 6.3); hidden-state effective rank (Sec 7). No verbalized numeric confidence and no trained probe; model_families_and_sizes=CodeLlama-7B-Instruct, DeepSeek-Coder-7B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct, OpenCoder-8B-Instruct (Sec 3.1); base_vs_instruct=yes, only OpenCoder and DeepSeek-Coder base vs instruct on MBPP+ (Sec 5.5, Fig 4, paired bootstrap on delta-AUC); post_training_stages=base vs instruction-tuned only (Sec 5.5); no RLHF/RL stage comparison; reasoning_models=not present (searched: reasoning, thinking, chain-of-thought; all four models non-reasoning); elicitation_formats=token-probability readouts; one prompted abstention format (answer only if more than 95% confident else 'I don't know') (Sec 6.3); no verbal-confidence format comparison; benchmarks=HumanEval+ (164), MBPP+ (378), BigCodeBench (1,140) (Sec 3.1); label_source=EvalPlus/BigCodeBench test-suite execution, pass/fail Z(x,y) (Sec 2.1); label_independence=labels from benchmark tests, independent of model confidence; construct-validity caveat in Sec 10; contamination_split=not present (searched: contaminat, release date, cutoff; Sec 10 threats do not mention contamination); calibration_metrics=AUC (Table 3), Brier score and Brier Skill Score (Table 5), selective accuracy vs coverage (Fig 3); no ECE found in the searched lines; granularity=whole program and token level (Sec 5.1-5.2); no per-requirement or per-test; causal_intervention=not present for hidden states (Sec 7 is correlational effective-rank comparison); calibration and prompting are interventions on readout only

## closing-the-confidence-faithfulness-gap-in-large-language-models
- [supports] Miao and Ungar find calibration and verbalized-confidence signals are linearly encoded but orthogonal across three open-weight models and four datasets, and that joint reasoning plus verbalization worsens miscalibration (Reasoning Contamination Effect); a two-stage steering pipeline improves calibration. | target: L2: probe-vs-verbal disconnect with causal steering exists outside code (NARROW overlap) | scope: no code, 7-8B models, MATH/MMLU/TriviaQA/TruthfulQA

## code-correctness-is-linearly-decodable-from-llm-hidden-states-before-generation
- [supports] Correctness of Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507's first-attempt code on 444 LiveCodeBench tasks is linearly decodable from the final-prompt-token hidden state before any output token, with leakage-free held-out AUC 0.881 ± 0.008 over 50 outer splits. | target: pre-generation probes occupy a facet of L2 | scope: Single model, LiveCodeBench, Table 3
- [supports] After residualizing each hidden dimension against prompt length the probe retains AUC 0.842 ± 0.010 versus a prompt-length-only baseline of 0.657 ± 0.014; nonlinear prompt-length models (RF 0.642, GB 0.649, MLP 0.657) do not exceed the logistic baseline. | target: confound control methodology for probes (difficulty/length) | scope: Tables 3-4
- [neutral] Layer choice is made by nested CV: layer 30 is modal (16 of 50 outer splits), layer 29 next (13), layer 33 (8); per-layer test AUC plateau 0.880-0.884 across layers 29-36. | target: probe protocol detail | scope: Tables 1-2
- [neutral] Self-repair data are starved: of 266 first-attempt failures only 20 recover within 5 attempts (14 at the first repair); conditional pass rate falls from 40.1% at attempt 0 to 5.3%, 2.0%, 0.4%, 0%. | target: L3 observed-failure update: repair signal null | scope: Sec 4; one 4B model; repair prompt includes verifier error output
- [supports] The paper states it studies one model only and defers cross-model replication; it positions itself against Bui et al. and Ribeiro et al., who read hidden states after generation. | target: L2 gap: pre-generation vs post-generation, multi-model replication left open | scope: Sec 1, Sec 4
  breadth: confidence_families=Linear probe on prompt-final hidden state only (PCA 95% + L2 logistic regression); prompt-length baselines (Sec 3.1); model_families_and_sizes=Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 only (36 layers, 2560-d) (Sec 2.1, Sec 4 'Single model'); base_vs_instruct=not present (searched: single instruct model); post_training_stages=not present (searched: single post-trained checkpoint); reasoning_models=not present (searched: model is the non-thinking Instruct-2507 variant; no reasoning-mode comparison); elicitation_formats=not present (no verbal or sampled readouts; hidden state at last prompt token before generation, Sec 2.2); benchmarks=444 LiveCodeBench tasks, easy/medium/hard (Sec 2.1); label_source=Unit-test pass/fail of first-attempt code (Sec 3.1); label_independence=Label is the probed model's own attempt-0 output (same model); contamination_split=LiveCodeBench cited as contamination-free benchmark (ref [6]) but no release-date vs cutoff split is run; 50 random stratified 80/20 splits with nested CV (Sec 3.1-3.2); calibration_metrics=AUC only: raw 0.881 ± 0.008, residualized 0.842 ± 0.010, prompt-length 0.657 ± 0.014 (Table 3); no ECE/Brier; granularity=Whole task (pre-generation, per prompt); causal_intervention=not present (searched: no steering/ablation; residualization against prompt length is a confound control only)

- [supports] Submit rate and test-verified resolve rate diverge: GPT-5 submits 100% and resolves 44%; Llama 4 99% vs 18%; Gemini submits 70% but resolves 50%, ranking differently from submit rate. | target: F: completion/indicator metrics mismeasure construct; instrument-validity framing for coding agents | scope: 50 SWE-bench Verified tasks, 5 runs each, 4 models, mini-SWE-agent, Table 1

## decomposing-probabilistic-scores-reliability-information-loss-and-uncertaintysup
- [supports] Calibration is a conditional property relative to an information level: the expected proper loss of an A-measurable predictor splits into a proper-regret (reliability) term and a conditional-entropy (residual uncertainty) term, with a chain decomposition for nested levels A subset B. | target: F: calibration is relative to an information level (instrument-conditional) | scope: Proper losses, population statements (Thm 2.1, 2.2)
- [supports] For classification with features X and score S=s(X), the loss splits into three terms: miscalibration, a grouping term measuring information loss from X to S, and irreducible uncertainty at the feature level. | target: F: distinguishes calibration error from information loss of the score (relevant to comparing verbal vs probe readouts) | scope: Sec 3, Prop 3.2 (calibration error as proper regret)
- [supports] Perfect calibration is defined as P(Y in . | S) = S almost surely, equivalently C = S, and post-hoc recalibration cannot recover information already lost in the compression X to S. | target: recalibration changes reliability but not grouping term | scope: Def 3.1; Intro
- [supports] The framework is applied to post-hoc recalibration, aggregation of calibrated models, and stagewise/boosting constructions, with explicit forms for Brier and log-loss; calibration is shown not generally preserved under aggregation of calibrated components. | target: aggregation of confidence readouts (ensembling verbal+probe) breaks calibration | scope: Sec 5 synthetic plus GermanCredit; not language models
- [neutral] The paper is a general statistical-learning result with no language models, no code, no execution labels; its relevance to a measurement-instrument framing (F) is as a decomposition toolkit, not as an occupant. | target: F is not occupied by this paper in the LLM-code setting | scope: Full-text grep: LLM 0, language model 0, code 0 relevant

## enhancing-automated-program-repair-via-faulty-token-localization-and-quality-awa
- [supports] TokenRepair uses context-aware token-level uncertainty fluctuations to localize likely-faulty tokens in a patch and rewrites only those, then filters low-quality patches by external feedback; it fixes 88 bugs on Defects4J 1.2 and 139 on HumanEval-Java. | target: L4 localization + targeted repair is partially occupied at token granularity (uncertainty-guided) | scope: Single-hunk APR, ground-truth hunk location given
- [supports] Token suspiciousness scores localize faulty tokens with average Top-K accuracy 0.589 to 0.695 across models and decay settings, evidence of a correlation between uncertainty scores and patch correctness. | target: L4 localization from model uncertainty | scope: Table 1 (Defects4J); Top-1 accuracy 0.405 to 0.484
- [supports] Ablation shows uncertainty-guided fault localization is the most influential component: removing it lowers performance by 20.6%. | target: L4 targeted repair vs regeneration | scope: RQ3 ablation, Table 5
- [neutral] The work motivates its signal by citing Spiess et al. 2024 that high-uncertainty code tokens are more often wrong, and relies on coarse test pass/fail as the external feedback it improves on. | target: prior art chain for token-level confidence | scope: Related work / Intro
- [neutral] Evaluation uses temperature 1, patch budget capped at 50, and the repair terminates when a plausible patch is found; no per-requirement or test-group confidence is used and no verbalized confidence is elicited. | target: L4 per-requirement/test-group confidence remains unoccupied here | scope: Sec 4.1.1
  breadth: confidence_families=Token-level uncertainty from output probabilities: suspiciousness score with decay factor alpha, plus patch-level quality metrics (RQ1, Table 1); model_families_and_sizes=Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, DeepSeek-Coder-6.7b-Instruct, DeepSeek-Coder-7b-Instruct-V1.5, CodeGemma-7b-it (Sec 4.1.1); base_vs_instruct=not present (searched: all five are instruct); post_training_stages=not present (searched: post-training comparison); reasoning_models=not present (searched: reasoning mode; none); elicitation_formats=Token log-prob uncertainty; Chain-of-Thought-guided rewriting; no verbalized confidence (searched: verbal 0); benchmarks=Defects4J 1.2 (154 single-hunk bugs) and HumanEval-Java (163 pairs) (Sec 4.1.2); label_source=Test execution plus manual verification of plausible patches by three researchers (Threats to Validity); label_independence=Fault location (buggy hunk) provided from ground truth (Sec 4.1.2); contamination_split=not present (searched: contaminat: 0 hits); calibration_metrics=Top-K localization accuracy (Top-1 about 0.41-0.48, avg 0.589-0.695, Table 1); correct-fix counts; no ECE; granularity=Token level within a patch; patch-level quality filter; causal_intervention=Ablation of components (RQ3): removing uncertainty-guided localization drops performance by 20.6%

## execre-execution-consistency-guided-reliability-estimation-for-self-correcting-c
- [supports] ExeCRE estimates code reliability by statistical analysis of execution-output consistency over many random inputs using a Dawid-Skene latent-reliability model, and uses it to gate self-correction; under GPT-5.2 on LiveCodeBench misleading feedback on already-correct code drops from 113.2 to 14.0. | target: L1/L3 execution-based reliability exists but is not calibration against ground truth | scope: LiveCodeBench v6 2025-01-01 to 2025-05-01, 182 problems
- [supports] The evaluation applies explicit release-window versus knowledge-cutoff control: the 182-problem window is cutoff-clean for Qwen2.5-Coder-32B-Instruct (cutoff 2024-06) and LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct (2023-12), but not for GPT-5.2 (2025-08); DeepSeek-V3.2 is unverifiable. | target: L1 release-date contamination control is practised for pass@1 in self-correction work, not for confidence calibration | scope: Sec 4.5, 5.1, Threats
- [supports] On cutoff-clean models ExeCRE raises Pass@1 modestly (Qwen2.5-Coder-32B 31.5 to 32.5; LLaMA-3.1-8B 19.4 to 21.0) while reducing misleading feedback (50.4 to 7.4; 30.0 to 1.2). | target: effect sizes on cutoff-clean subset | scope: Sec 5.1
- [supports] The paper declines to treat GPT-5.2 and DeepSeek-V3.2 results as contamination-free evidence and notes no cutoff-clean subset exists for GPT-5.2 in the latest LiveCodeBench release available to the authors. | target: contamination caveat for frontier models | scope: Sec 5.1

## functional-entropy-predicting-functional-correctness-in-llm-generated-code-with
- [supports] NLI-based semantic entropy collapses to near-chance AUROC on code (Python 0.50-0.53, Java 0.48-0.56, SQL 0.47-0.58) because NLI cannot separate functionally different code. | target: L1: execution-graded UQ for code is OCCUPIED for closed API models | scope: GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, Gemini-2.5-Pro/Flash/Flash-Lite; LiveCodeBench, MultiPL-E Java, LiveSQLBench
- [supports] LLM-judged functional equivalence methods (functional entropy, equivalence rate, sets) achieve top AUROC in 11 of 15 model-benchmark combinations, up to 0.867 on LiveCodeBench; verbalized confidence is erratic. | target: L1: sampling-based functional clustering is the strong baseline | scope: m=10 samples at T=1.0; judge Gemini-2.5-Flash validated at 93-97% precision against execution
- [supports] No open-weight models, hidden-state probes, base/instruct pairs, or interventions are evaluated; all five models are closed APIs. | target: L2: hidden-state vs verbal comparison on open code models is not covered here | scope: Sec 4.1 setup; Limitations
- [supports] Execution-triggered verification is named only as future work; confidence-routed execution budgets are not tested. | target: L3: routing utility not tested here | scope: Conclusion

## give-up-to-get-more-priors-not-policies-govern-test-time-compute-allocation
- [supports] The best published difficulty-proportional allocation rule captures half or more of the oracle gain over uniform allocation on workloads the model mostly solves, but at most 13% on hard workloads even when given true pass rates; on CodeContests with Llama-3-8B (mean p 0.021) IAS captures 1% and with Llama-3-70B (mean p 0.074) 2%. | target: L3 budget-routing vs oracle is partly occupied for pass-rate signals | scope: Offline replay; Table 1, u=16 attempts/problem
- [supports] With a verifier and a prior from a related model's past runs on the same inputs, Thompson Sampling raises the solved share on the hardest workload from 14% to 27%; without such a prior no tested policy beats round-robin, and the gain vanishes between prior rank correlations of about 0.3 and 0.55. | target: information (prior quality), not policy, drives routing gain | scope: Sec 5; verifier required; novel inputs get no gain
- [supports] Cheap priors (text regression, LLM-derived features, zero-shot LLM difficulty judgments, dissimilar-model history) correlate weakly with true pass rates and give no gain over round-robin (at most 1.7 points at 16 attempts per problem). | target: a model-internal or verbal confidence prior must reach rank correlation above about 0.3-0.55 to help; verbal/probe confidence untested here | scope: Table 3
- [supports] The gain requires a verifier whose false acceptance rate is well below the workload pass rate: it survives 20% false rejections but all but vanishes at a 5% false acceptance rate. | target: verifier-quality dependency relevant to self-written tests (L4) and execution budgets (L3) | scope: Sec 5 verifier-error model
- [neutral] A survey of 221 LLM allocation papers finds at least 13 (5.9%) give the hardest inputs the most attempts, with 4 more borderline; one surveyed rule samples until a confidence threshold is met so low-confidence inputs consume more samples. | target: literature-map for L3 | scope: Sec 3; LLM-assisted coding, authors treat 13 as lower bound
- [neutral] The paper explicitly makes no claim about learned verifiers, novel user queries, open-ended generation, or frontier reasoning models. | target: scope limits leave verbal/probe-confidence routing unexamined | scope: Intro

## hidden-error-awareness-in-chain-of-thought-reasoningthe-signal-is-diagnostic-not
- [supports] A linear hidden-state probe predicts CoT trace correctness at 0.95 AUROC, far above verbalized confidence (0.67), self-consistency (0.82), and CCS (0.72), on math reasoning. | target: L2 probe-beats-verbal pattern generalizes outside code (math) and outside non-reasoning models | scope: MATH-500 (100 train, 200 held-out); nine models 1.5B-72B; Sec 3, Tables 1-2
- [supports] Four causal interventions (activation steering, probe-guided best-of-N, self-correction, activation patching) fail to improve accuracy; the probe signal is diagnostic, not causal. | target: L2: causal-intervention component for probes is occupied in math, not code | scope: Sec 5; MATH-500

- [neutral] Stated limitations: MATH-500 is the primary benchmark, models up to 72B, one reasoning model, no probe-based fine-tuning. | target: Gap left for code | scope: Sec 7 Limitations

## measurement-validity-and-representation-dynamics-ofconfidence-signals-in-large-l
- [supports] A pre-registered audit of verbal, probe, and sampling-agreement confidence on five Qwen2.5 models (0.5B-7B, one base/instruct pair at 7B) finds three formats of verbal confidence barely correlate (Spearman 0.126, -0.069, 0.247 vs target >=0.60). | target: F: measurement-validity framing of confidence is OCCUPIED for factual and math tasks (not code) | scope: Qwen2.5 only; PopQA, SimpleQA, GSM8K, MATH; no code
- [supports] ECE misleads: verbal confidence has low ECE by clustering near the base rate, while sampling agreement has worse ECE but about 2.02x the Murphy resolution and the lowest Brier. | target: F: ECE alone is an invalid instrument; resolution must be reported | scope: N=842 matched items, 10 cells, base rate 0.2815
- [supports] Probe onset layers for predicting sampling-inconsistency labels are earlier for retrieval (mean 6.82%) than for reasoning (mean 28.08%) of depth, but in 9 of 11 retrieval cells a prompt-only TF-IDF baseline matches the probe. | target: L2: probe-versus-surface-baseline controls exist outside code | scope: Probe label is semantic-entropy median split, not correctness; last prompt token only

## measuring-what-ai-systems-might-do-towards-a-measurement-science-in-ai
- [supports] Capabilities and propensities are dispositional properties, characterized by counterfactual relationships between contextual conditions and behavioural outputs, and are routinely conflated with observed performance. | target: F: measurement-science framing of AI evaluation exists at the conceptual level | scope: Conceptual paper, philosophy of measurement; Feb 2026
- [supports] Measuring a disposition requires (i) hypothesising causally relevant contextual properties, (ii) independently operationalising and measuring them, and (iii) empirically mapping how variation in them affects the probability of the behaviour; benchmark averages and latent-variable models like IRT bypass these steps. | target: F: three-step measurement recipe, a direct framing occupant for AI evaluation (not calibration of confidence) | scope: Abstract; Sec 5
- [supports] The paper contrasts convenience benchmarking with calibrated instruments, likening the needed shift to going from touching an object to feel warmth to the invention of calibrated thermometers, and cites Tal on calibration as modelling the measurement process. | target: F: thermometer analogy is occupied rhetorically in AI evaluation literature | scope: Sec 1/Conclusion; ref Tal (2017) Calibration: modelling the measurement process
- [supports] Benchmarks historically arose as engineering tests or challenge problems rather than theoretically grounded measurement instruments, so benchmark aggregates should not be read as measurements of dispositions. | target: F: construct-validity critique of benchmarks | scope: Footnote 2, Sec 1
- [neutral] The paper does not address confidence estimation, calibration error of model confidence, code, or execution-graded labels; it does not use metrology terms (searched: metrolog 0 hits) and thus leaves an instrument-framed calibration study of code-LLM confidence unoccupied. | target: F is NARROW-to-CLEAN against this paper for confidence calibration | scope: Full-text grep: metrolog 0, reliability 0, code 5 incidental

- [supports] A RepE/LAT correctness direction from last-token hidden states outperforms intrinsic likelihood and verbalized (reflective) confidence at picking the correct implementation among four candidates on HumanEval and BigCodeBench for four 7-8B instruct LLMs. | target: L2 probe-beats-verbal on code is occupied | scope: Four 7-8B Instruct models; candidate-selection accuracy not calibration (Sec 4.2.1)
- [supports] Table 3 (BigCodeBench) in-distribution LAT(Val) accuracies are Mistral 51.1% +/-10.6, Qwen 54.4% +/-3.8, CodeLlama 50.3% +/-6.2, OpenCoder 56.3% +/-5.1, against Random 27.5% (Mistral) and verbalized baselines 23.7-41.1%. | target: L2 occupancy numbers | scope: Table 3, Test_BCB, 4 candidates per task
- [supports] Verbalized (reflective) confidence is inconsistent across models and often no better than random in this setup; on HumanEval reflective ranking often does worse than Random. | target: verbal readouts weak on code | scope: Sec 5.2.2 HumanEval pass@rank-k ranking
- [neutral] Out-of-distribution fitting of the correctness direction is erratic: synthetic fitting drops below random for Qwen (12.2%) and CodeLlama (22.2%) on BigCodeBench, MBPP+ fitting stays above random on HumanEval (32.2%-39.9%) but below in-distribution (41.4-60.7%). | target: probe transfer limits | scope: Sec 4.2.2
- [supports] Ranking 10 self-samples by LAT lifts pass@rank-1 above pass@1 without test execution; e.g. Mistral HumanEval Fit_HE reaches 41.3% at rank-1 versus 34.7% pass@1 and RankEF 35.5%; the paper reports only a few comparisons were statistically significant. | target: probe-based ranking (selection utility, not budget routing) is occupied at whole-solution level | scope: Sec 5.2; test execution used only for evaluation
- [supports] The label is test-execution outcome, but the paper reports no calibration metric (no ECE, AUROC, Brier) for any confidence family and does not compare base to instruct, reasoning to non-reasoning, or do any causal intervention. | target: L2 sub-components base/instruct, reasoning, causal are not occupied by this paper | scope: Sec 6.3, 7; negatives from grep of full text (AUROC 0, steer 0, contaminat 0)
- [neutral] Authors position prior work Bui et al. (2025) as probing single-program correctness with a separate classifier, and Huang et al. (2025) as line-level risk assessment, differing from their ranking setup. | target: prior-art map for L2/L4 | scope: Sec 8 Related Work
